Michael Danylchuk
fix: critical RLS, legacy encryption, KMM live-query, and transaction races (#256)
* fix(security): close RLS batch and purge bypasses

* fix(security): avoid purge policy reentrancy

* fix(security): enforce RLS in deprecated async CRUD

* fix(security): preserve legacy encrypted database access

* fix(kmm): reuse open handles for live queries

* fix(transactions): serialize snapshot and rollback state

Take the client write lock around begin/commit/rollback and snapshot or
restore collection state under the DynamicCollection barrier so concurrent
readers cannot observe half-rolled-back indexes or race Dictionary mutation.

981bbccd9990f148fbed6a919af43f7a88262538
2026-05-16
Michael Danylchuk
feat(cli): new REPL UX and master keyring workflows (#197)
* feat(cli): add interactive shell UX and master keyring workflows

Ship the new blazedb start experience with rich terminal rendering, picker navigation, and REPL integration while introducing secure master keyring commands (init/add/list/remove/status) with scoped secret storage.

* fix(cli): resolve Linux Swift 6.2 CI compile failures

Remove non-portable stdout/stderr and OSStatus usage from CLI shell helpers so the master keyring and terminal rendering code compiles cleanly under strict Swift 6.2 Linux CI checks.

* fix(cli): remove remaining stdout global in REPL banner

Replace the final fflush(stdout) call in the REPL welcome path with the terminal draw abstraction to satisfy Swift 6.2 strict concurrency checks on Linux CI.

* fix(cli): replace remaining stderr global in BlazedbEntry

The unknown-option error branch still called `fputs(..., stderr)`, which
Linux Swift 6.2/6.3 reject under strict concurrency because `extern FILE
*stderr` from glibc is treated as non-Sendable shared mutable state. This
broke the `blazedb` product build in the Tier 0 Linux CI job and blocked
every downstream test.

Route those two writes through a small `writeStderrLine` helper backed by
`FileHandle.standardError`, matching the pattern already used elsewhere
(e.g. BlazeShell/CLIPasswordReader) and the earlier 1b79d86 / b3a287b
sweeps.

Verified locally on Linux aarch64 (Swift 6.3):
- `swift build --product blazedb` succeeds
- `blazedb --bogus-flag` exits 1 and writes the message to stderr
- `swift test --filter CLIMasterKeyringTests` passes
- `swift test --filter CLIDiscoveryTests.testPickerRenderColumnsAlign` passes

The unknown-option error branch still called `fputs(..., stderr)`, which
Linux Swift 6.2/6.3 reject under strict concurrency because `extern FILE
*stderr` from glibc is treated as non-Sendable shared mutable state. This
broke the `blazedb` product build in the Tier 0 Linux CI job and blocked
every downstream test.

`FileHandle.standardError`, matching the pattern already used elsewhere
(e.g. BlazeShell/CLIPasswordReader) and the earlier 1b79d86 / b3a287b
sweeps.

- `swift build --product blazedb` succeeds
- `blazedb --bogus-flag` exits 1 and writes the message to stderr

---COMMIT---
c9691567f3cfeda745b148954feaad8ceec6899d
2026-05-02
Michael Danylchuk
fix(package): remove unsafeFlags from BlazeDBCore so consumers can depend on it
SwiftPM blocks consumer packages from depending on any product whose
targets use .unsafeFlags(...), regardless of whether the flags would
actually apply (the check runs at manifest-evaluation time, not at
build time). The flag here was a debug-only workaround for a Swift
6.0.x IRGen abort during SwiftUI Binding<> debug reconstruction in
BlazeQuery+Extensions.swift.

Removing it lets downstream packages (e.g. AshPileServer) depend on
BlazeDB on both macOS and Linux. If a consumer hits the underlying
IRGen bug on a specific Swift 6.0.x toolchain, they can add the same
-Xfrontend -disable-round-trip-debug-types flag to their own target.

M	Package.swift

fix: legacy DynamicCollection.update uses barrier on Linux (#81) (#177)
Legacy non-MVCC update() skipped queue.sync(flags: .barrier) under

mutation of indexMap. Align update with insert/delete by always running
_updateNoSync under a barrier.

Adds regression test: concurrent fetchAll vs update on one record.

5ae6cb30db80c5708095216014d57b3c316f42df
2026-04-11
Michael Danylchuk
ci(nightly): Linux Tier2 core only; remove nightly extended + Tier3 (#149)
* ci(nightly): keep Linux Tier2 core only; drop extended + Tier3 from nightly

Remove BlazeDB_Tier2_Extended and Tier3 heavy/perf commands from scheduled nightly.

Made-with: Cursor

* ci(nightly): trim redundant macOS Tier3/Tier1 and Linux Tier0 (#148)

* test(tier3): reduce CPU profiling fixture size for CI stability

The Tier3 heavy profiling lane was failing intermittently due to excessive setup volume in the CPU-intensive query benchmark. Scale fixture size by environment and assert the exact expected result cardinality so runtime drops substantially without weakening query correctness guarantees.

* test(tier3): clarify CPU profiling expected-count logic

Make the cardinality expectation in CPUIntensiveQuery explicit and reader-friendly, document why CI/local fixture sizes differ, and keep all mirrored profiling test copies aligned to prevent drift.

* ci(nightly): quarantine macOS tier3 lane and add diagnostics

Keep nightly correctness gates unblocked by the unstable Tier3 profiling lane while preserving visibility. Add explicit non-blocking lane labeling and automated post-run diagnostics so failures are classified and easier to triage.

* fix(tier3): lighten XCTest profiling on GitHub Actions for stable nightly Tier3

Tier3 measure() blocks were driving pathological memory peaks and process
exit without assertion failures on hosted runners. On GITHUB_ACTIONS, use
single measure iterations, omit or replace XCTMemoryMetric where it
amplified load, and scale down the heaviest baseline fixtures. Local runs
keep full iterations and full metrics.

Document the CI-vs-local policy and pre-refute naive restorations of
multi-iteration profiling on CI. Align nightly Tier3 job timeout with the
lighter lane intent and clarify workflow comments.

Made-with: Cursor

* ci(nightly): remove redundant macOS Tier3/Tier1 and Linux Tier0 jobs

Drop nightly-macos-tier3-heavy (non-blocking, duplicated by weekly/deep).
Drop nightly-macos-tier1 (duplicates PR macOS Tier1).
Drop nightly-linux-tier0 (duplicates PR Linux Tier0).
Update CI_AND_TEST_TIERS.md to match nightly.yml.

Made-with: Cursor

* fix(ci): restore main nightly Linux split; drop only redundant jobs

PR #148 intended to remove nightly-macos-tier3-heavy, nightly-macos-tier1,
and linux-tier0. Merge conflict resolution had replaced nightly.yml with an
older combined-Linux shape. Re-apply those three deletions on top of
origin/main so split linux-tier2-* / linux-tier3-* jobs and naming from
#142–#146 remain.

fix(tier3): stabilize XCTest profiling on GitHub Actions (#141)
* test(tier3): reduce CPU profiling fixture size for CI stability

The Tier3 heavy profiling lane was failing intermittently due to excessive setup volume in the CPU-intensive query benchmark. Scale fixture size by environment and assert the exact expected result cardinality so runtime drops substantially without weakening query correctness guarantees.

* test(tier3): clarify CPU profiling expected-count logic

Make the cardinality expectation in CPUIntensiveQuery explicit and reader-friendly, document why CI/local fixture sizes differ, and keep all mirrored profiling test copies aligned to prevent drift.

* ci(nightly): quarantine macOS tier3 lane and add diagnostics

Keep nightly correctness gates unblocked by the unstable Tier3 profiling lane while preserving visibility. Add explicit non-blocking lane labeling and automated post-run diagnostics so failures are classified and easier to triage.

* fix(tier3): lighten XCTest profiling on GitHub Actions for stable nightly Tier3

Tier3 measure() blocks were driving pathological memory peaks and process
exit without assertion failures on hosted runners. On GITHUB_ACTIONS, use
single measure iterations, omit or replace XCTMemoryMetric where it
amplified load, and scale down the heaviest baseline fixtures. Local runs
keep full iterations and full metrics.

Document the CI-vs-local policy and pre-refute naive restorations of
multi-iteration profiling on CI. Align nightly Tier3 job timeout with the
lighter lane intent and clarify workflow comments.

406f330f725a9bc3373ec69f869feb0f8819ac19
2026-04-09
Michael Danylchuk
test-tiers: reclassify legacy Tier1Extended/Tier1Perf under Tier2/Tier3 ownership (#110)
* fix(core): snapshot unique-field sets under lock in validateUnique

- Avoid iterating uniqueFields/uniqueCompoundFields while another thread mutates them.
- Docs: add CI failure triage (first artifact, not last passer).
- ci(nightly): raise macOS confidence job timeout to 480m (was 360m) for Tier1+FastFull+Tier2+verify.

* ci(nightly): split confidence lane by failure domain

Break nightly into isolated macOS jobs for Tier1 depth, extra-package Tier1FastFull, strict Tier2, clean-checkout, and README quickstart while keeping TSan and Linux as separate axes. Remove the duplicate combined GoldenPath verification step and refresh nightly docs to match the new rerunnable topology.

* ci(nightly): root-own Tier1Fast and Tier2 confidence lanes

Make nightly confidence independent of BlazeDBExtraTests by running the macOS Tier1 lane as BlazeDB_Tier1Fast and promoting BlazeDB_Tier2 to a first-class root test target used by run-tier2.sh. Remove remaining clean-checkout dependency on BlazeDBExtraTests and update nightly docs so lane names match executed coverage.

* ci(deep): untether Tier3 lanes from BlazeDBExtraTests

Root-own Tier3 heavy/destructive targets and switch deep-validation, release deep run, and tier3 scripts to run from the root package so CI no longer depends on BlazeDBExtraTests manifest validity. Update tier docs and contributor guidance to reflect root-owned Tier2/Tier3 execution paths.

* test-tiers: canonicalize Tier1 naming across active paths

Rename BlazeDB_Tier1Fast to BlazeDB_Tier1 in the root manifest and align active workflows, scripts, and maintainer docs with a single canonical Tier1 target. Remove active FastFull language, preserve legacy Tier1Extended/Tier1Perf as explicit pre-PR3 lanes, and keep runtime behavior unchanged for tier1-depth assets.

* test-tiers: reclassify legacy Tier1 depth suites as transitional Tier2/Tier3 companions

Retire BlazeDB_Tier1Extended and BlazeDB_Tier1Perf names while preserving coverage under Tier2/Tier3 ownership using temporary companion targets, and make workflows/docs explicitly describe this as a PR3 bridge pending PR4 normalization.

eaf8dfedd3558edb35b89a9e8c2b97a267269afe
2026-04-08
Michael Danylchuk
cleanup: remove redundant `try` in `try? try` expressions across tests (#102)
`try? try expr` is semantically identical to `try? expr` — the outer
`try?` already converts any thrown error to nil, making the inner `try`
redundant. Same for `try? await try` → `try? await`.

22 instances fixed across 10 test files.

83f4672a51a6223cc93fddcea779ddf4c2e07f33
2026-04-08
Michael Danylchuk
CI: fix ThreadSanitizer invocation and upgrade upload-artifact to v5 (#97)
Two issues discovered in Nightly confidence #9:

1. TSan step crashes with "Interceptors are not working" because
   `-Xswiftc -sanitize=thread` only instruments the compiler without
   loading the TSan runtime early enough. SwiftPM's built-in flag
   `--sanitize thread` handles both instrumentation and runtime setup.
   Fixed in nightly.yml, release.yml, and deep-validation.yml (4 sites).

2. actions/upload-artifact@v4 targets deprecated Node.js 20, producing
   warnings on every run. Upgraded to @v5 across all 6 workflow files
   (13 occurrences).
M	.github/workflows/ci.yml

---COMMIT---
21723bd7e34793043fc13dba03171765ec8455f2
2026-04-08
Michael Danylchuk
fix: synchronize monitoring reads against collection queue to prevent data race (#95)
getStorageInfo(), getPerformanceInfo(), getSchemaInfo(), and
getRecordCount() read collection.indexMap, nextPageIndex,
secondaryIndexes, and mvccEnabled without going through the
collection's GCD concurrent queue. Under concurrent access
(e.g. testObservability_DoesNotDeadlock) this produces torn reads
that crash with NSInvalidArgumentException ("-[__NSCFNumber count]:
unrecognized selector"). Wrap all mutable-state reads in
collection.queue.sync { } — the same pattern already used by
DynamicCollection.fetch(id:), fetchAll(), contains(), and
QueryStatistics.

Fixes nightly confidence #8 crash on macOS 15.
M	BlazeDB/Exports/BlazeDBClient+Monitoring.swift

---COMMIT---
61a16b127c285f91584cdcbfcf4cec0d029b675d
2026-04-08
Michael Danylchuk
Fix groupBy field validation bypassed on cold-cache fetchAll in CI
validateGroupByFields() uses try? collection.fetchAll() and silently skips
validation when the result is empty. For groupBy-only queries (no sort
operations), validateSortFields() never calls fetchAll(), leaving the cache
cold. On CI (macOS-15) the cold _fetchAllOptimized() path can return []
before the cache is warm, causing validation to be skipped entirely.
_executeGroupedAggregation() then groups 0 records and returns successfully
instead of throwing, so the test hits XCTFail("Should have thrown error").

Fix: add authoritative field validation inside _executeGroupedAggregation
after fetching the actual records. This fires regardless of cache state,
making it immune to the cold-cache bypass.

M	BlazeDB/Query/QueryBuilder.swift

---COMMIT---
4b24ca058cd0c93e4821988dd0b464d5105ae0fa
2026-04-08
Michael Danylchuk
fix: validate groupBy fields at execution time to fix Linux CI failures
The pre-execution validateGroupByFields() in validateQuery() is best-effort:
it uses try? collection.fetchAll() and skips validation if the result is empty.
On Linux, fetchAllBasic() silently drops records whose page-fetches fail, so
the sample can be empty even in a populated database. When validation is skipped,
_executeGroupedAggregation() runs aggregateGrouped() which silently groups
missing fields as "null" — no error thrown, tests hit XCTFail.

Fix: add definitive groupBy field validation inside _executeGroupedAggregation()
after the authoritative fetchAll() call, using the same records that the
aggregation will use. This check fires regardless of whether the best-effort
pre-validation fired. Also widen findSimilarFields() to internal so both
call sites can share it.

Fixes testInvalidGroupByField_FailsWithHelpfulMessage and
testErrorMessagesIncludeGuidance (QueryErgonomicsTests lines 63 and 112).

M	BlazeDB/Query/QueryBuilder+Validation.swift
M	BlazeDB/Query/QueryBuilder.swift

- Add resolveStorage() throws as a documented throwing alternative
- Add Tier1 tests for error propagation and successful storage parity

---COMMIT---
79180935d2e5eef562c57f87db7c35e2c899152b
2026-04-08
Michael Danylchuk
fix(ci): always rebuild Tier1Perf (drop --skip-build in step 6) (#87)
Nightly macOS job builds BlazeDBCore, runs Tier1Extended, then ran
`swift test --skip-build --filter BlazeDB_Tier1Perf`. Tier1Extended does
not build the Tier1Perf test target, so the perf bundle often came from
restored .build cache and could execute against stale core/query code.
That produced failures matching pre-#86 GROUP BY validation despite main
already shipping the fix.

Remove --skip-build for Tier1Perf in nightly, tier1-depth, and
deep-validation workflows.
M	.github/workflows/deep-validation.yml
M	.github/workflows/nightly.yml
M	.github/workflows/tier1-depth.yml

4b75503d28248c75ac4a7684448ffdcadc4a4dfe
2026-04-07
Michael Danylchuk
docs: add canonical SYSTEM_MAP.md (#76)
* docs: add canonical SYSTEM_MAP.md and change discipline rules

Create a single engineering source of truth for what exists, what
state it is in, and where it lives. Grounded in Package.swift, source
files, tests, and open issues — not aspirations.

Covers: core engine, storage/durability, transactions, encryption,
typed models, queries, indexing, schema/migration, SwiftUI, distributed
(deferred), telemetry (deferred), CLI tools, examples, platform support,
CI lanes, and known blockers.

Adds a "Change Discipline" section to CONTRIBUTING.md covering system
map update rules, branch/scope discipline, feature surface rules, and
reconciliation policy.

Relates to #58.

* docs: link SYSTEM_MAP.md from README documentation table

* docs: fix system map accuracy against current main

Corrections from deep code audit:
- BlazeDataRecord is in Exports/BlazeTypes.swift, not Core/BlazeRecord.swift
- Triggers have public APIs (registerTrigger, onInsert, etc.), not "Internal/no public API"
- Tier1Fast has many exclusions (PR #72 not yet merged), not "3 files excluded"
- MVCC, export, stats, health code locations now point to actual extension files
- Record encoding paths include Core/ prefix consistently

Additions:
- Security subsystem: certificate pinning, security context/policy/auditor, user context, key unlock
- Storage: PageReuseGC (distinct from vacuum), StorageManager (public cleanup/disk APIs)
- Core: BlazeRecordDSL (public record builder)
- Migration: MigrationProgressMonitor (excluded from BlazeDBCore)
- Distributed: BlazeServer, BlazeOperation, SecurityValidator, 7-file GC suite

* docs: second accuracy audit of SYSTEM_MAP.md

Path prefix consistency:
- WALEntry, CompressionSupport, WindowFunctions, PolicyEngine,
  AccessManager, KeyUnlockProvider, MigrationExecutor, ForeignKeys,
  CheckConstraints, BlazeOperation+BlazeBinary all now include
  their directory prefix consistently.

Status/surface corrections:
- Record builder DSL: Stable/Public → Internal/Public with caveats
  (not used in any example, test, or onboarding doc)
- Storage manager: Internal/Public with caveats → Partial/Public with
  caveats (resolves glossary contradiction; has public API but no docs)
- Triggers code location: remove Exports/BlazeDBClient+Triggers.swift
  (has no public symbols); public API is in Core/Triggers.swift

Missing entries:
- Distributed client exports (Discovery, Sync, Telemetry, SharedSecret)
  excluded from BlazeDBCore but not previously tracked

* docs: deep surface census — add 5 missing subsystems to SYSTEM_MAP

New rows:
- Client lifecycle (open/close/validate): 7 files, 36 public
  symbols — the "how do I get a db handle" surface, previously
  invisible
- Multi-database manager (BlazeDBManager): public singleton with
  mount/unmount/switch, used by CLI tools
- Change observation: db.observe() pub/sub with DatabaseChange,
  ObserverToken, BlazeDBSnapshot (Observability/ directory)
- Monitoring framework: 69 public symbols — DatabaseMonitoringSnapshot
  with 5 info subtypes, the largest single public API surface
- BlazeBinary codec location fix: actual encoder/decoder is in
  Utils/ (18 files), not Core/

Corrections:
- Spatial/vector queries: upgraded from Internal to Public with
  caveats — client APIs exist (enableSpatialIndex, etc.)
- Transactions: added Transactions/ directory + savepoints note
- CRUD: added batch upsert mention
- Stats/health: added interpretation and limits extensions
- Encoding: fixed code location to cite Utils/BlazeBinary*

Surfaces intentionally NOT added (noise):
- ~5 Exports files (error presentation, telemetry stub, AI, workspace)
- ~16 DynamicCollection+*.swift extensions (fold under existing rows)
- Testing/ (2 files — CI detection + data seeding)
- Internal/ (1 file — internal migration helper)
- BlazeDB.docc (DocC bundle)
- BlazeCollection (deprecated), BlazeQueryContext (legacy)

* docs: truth extraction — expose hidden behavioral truths in SYSTEM_MAP

Add 1 missing row (BlazeDBError unified error model) and expand 13
existing rows with behavioral truth that was previously hidden behind
accurate-sounding summaries:

- TypedStore: map-not-compactMap throws on multi-type DBs; count() is
  total-DB count
- CRUD: fetchAll() silently drops decode failures via try?
- Query planner: vector/FTS/hybrid strategies fall back to sequential
  scan; regularIndex still does fetchAll + filter
- Argon2 KDF: dead code; actual production KDF is PBKDF2-SHA256 600k
  iterations
- Key management: PBKDF2 details, iteration counts, legacy salt caveat
- Backup/export: JSON format, Linux base64 fallback (not cryptographic)
- WAL replay: uncommitted txns silently discarded; mid-WAL corruption
  is hard failure
- Page cache: O(n) LRU eviction, not configurable
- GCD threading: placeholder queue label, 5s static fetchAll cache
- MVCC GC: holds VersionManager lock — blocks all MVCC reads/writes
- B-tree index: not consumed by query execution path
- SwiftUI wrappers: hard dependency on ChangeObservation subsystem
- BlazeStorable/BlazeDocument: no bridge protocol between them
- Linux platform: BLAZEDB_LINUX_CORE gates 68 files, not just SwiftUI

f2f6c90d3c8f16ea1dbd77fc824f5a76534d1513
2026-03-14
Michael Danylchuk
fix: harden transaction recovery boundaries and release readiness (#17)
* fix(core): namespace transaction logs and lock manager state

Use per-database transaction log paths with legacy fallback so recovery cannot collide across databases sharing a directory. Add coarse-grained state locking to BlazeDBManager to make mounted database bookkeeping thread-safe.

* fix(wal): commit unified writes before applying staged pages

Preserve crash atomicity in unified mode by staging unsynchronized page writes in memory, committing WAL first, then applying staged pages to the main file. Ensure synchronize/checkpoint/close flush staged writes after durable commit.

* docs(ci): align release policy, tier checks, and version guidance

Update release workflow to run declared tier targets and refresh release/support documentation to the current 2.x line. Remove stale beta-era guidance and document vX.Y.Z tagging policy consistently across contributor and release docs.

2026-03-14
Michael Danylchuk
fix(core/security): harden salt handling and align security tests (#7)
* fix(core): harden record conversion and WAL bounds checks

Improve Codable numeric/bool field conversion, remove JSON round-trips in BlazeCollection encoding, and guard WAL page index casting to UInt32 to prevent invalid writes.

* fix(security): use per-database salt in metadata and backup flows

Replace hardcoded metadata salt usage with each database's kdfSalt and make backup sidecar replacement best-effort to reduce race failures in concurrent test environments.

* test(security): align coverage with key isolation and current API behavior

Update recovery, backup, migration, and security tests to use isolated temp artifacts, enforce wrong-password failures, and reflect non-throwing/idempotent API contracts.

* fix(swift6): enforce MainActor refresh and QueryResult sendability

Wrap SwiftUI refresh triggers in MainActor tasks and mark QueryResult as @unchecked Sendable so async query execution compiles under Swift 6 concurrency checks.

* fix(swift6): remove captured var mutation in parallel BlazeBinary paths

Replace shared mutable arrays in parallel encode/decode helpers with locked reference containers so concurrent closures no longer mutate captured vars under Swift 6 strict concurrency checks.

* fix(ci): resolve cross-platform APIs, concurrency misuse, and deterministic dump issues

* ci: simplify pipeline to single Build & Test gate; move deep tests to nightly/manual

* fix(tests): make macOS memory probe Swift 6 concurrency-safe

* fix(tests): unblock Swift 6 PR gate concurrency diagnostics

* fix(tests): resolve Swift 6 actor-isolation errors in Tier1 helpers

* fix(tests): make foreign key thread-safety closure sendable-safe

* ci: use runner-native Swift toolchain on macOS 15

* fix(core): harden secure random handling and Linux observability state

Centralize random byte generation with strict behavior for KDF salt creation and route observability metrics through shared associated-object storage to resolve Swift 6/Linux concurrency issues without committing unintended workspace mutations.

ce2301f0012ce56ad5eca0f6dea6693680db02fb
2026-03-14
Michael Danylchuk
feat(wal): wire BlazeTransaction to DurabilityManager in unified mode
In unified mode, BlazeTransaction now uses DurabilityManager for
transaction boundaries (begin/commit/abort). Commit path enforces
WAL-before-page-write: encrypt → WAL writes → WAL commit (fsync) →
pwrite pages → fsync main. Single fsync per transaction (batch
optimization).

Rollback in unified mode discards staged pages without baseline
restore since pages are never written before commit.

PageStore gains _encryptPageBuffer and _writeEncryptedBuffer internal
methods to support the split encrypt/write flow.

TransactionContext branches legacy/unified paths. Legacy path unchanged.
7 new tests, 58 total tests green.

Phase 1, Chunk 3 Task 5 of WAL consolidation.

f994fc2ba13bc9fedff13ddd52646f120295f97d
2026-03-14
Michael Danylchuk
fix: WALEntry memory safety, error labels, and test coverage
Apply 7 critical fixes to WALEntry implementation:

1. Fix bindMemory UB in computeCRC32: Replace buf.bindMemory with
   buf.baseAddress!.assumingMemoryBound(to: UInt8.self) for correct
   alignment-free memory access per Swift UnsafeRawBufferPointer semantics.

2. Swap inverted CRC mismatch error labels: now correctly reports
   crcMismatch(expected: computed, actual: stored) per error semantics.

3. Add guard for offset < data.startIndex in readNext to prevent
   underflow on negative or invalid offsets.

4. Wrap computeCRC32 in #if canImport(zlib) with fatalError fallback
   since CRC32 is a hard requirement for WAL integrity.

5. Replace fragile string matching in CRC test with guard-case pattern
   matching on WALError.crcMismatch enum case.

6. Add testReadNextSequential to validate sequential entry decoding and
   offset tracking across multiple entries.

7. Add testZeroLSNRoundTrip to ensure LSN=0 boundary case works correctly.

All 6 tests pass (now includes 2 new tests). Build clean.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.6 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

  data.count - offset in guard statements. This ensures correct behavior when
  data is a slice with startIndex != 0.
- Fixed Issue 2: Added .delete operation to the testBeginCommitAbortCheckpointRoundTrip
  test to ensure all WAL operation types are tested.

All WALEntryTests pass successfully.

2026-03-14
Michael Danylchuk
feat: add WALEntry binary format with CRC32 validation
New WAL entry schema with LSN, transactionID, operation type,
pageIndex, and payload. Supports begin/write/delete/commit/abort/
checkpoint operations. CRC32 validated on deserialize.

Phase 1, Task 1 of WAL consolidation.

---COMMIT---
0df38a72b6cb6263bc290ee1271ccb31cd734935
2026-03-13
Michael Danylchuk
fix: use UUID instanceID for fetchAll cache key instead of ObjectIdentifier
ObjectIdentifier uses the memory address of DynamicCollection, which can
be reused after deallocation. When a new collection lands at the same
address, the 5-second TTL cache returns stale results from the previous
collection — causing insertMany records to be invisible to queries.

Fix: add a stable UUID instanceID to DynamicCollection, use it as the
cache dictionary key instead of ObjectIdentifier.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.6 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

- RLS: Change userOwnsRecord/userInTeam policies from restrictive to
  permissive (they grant access, not restrict it). Split viewerReadOnly
  into restrictive deny-all + permissive select companion.
- Schema: Add createdAt to auto-injected fields skip set in strict mode

  concurrent insert and retrying as update.

  file-not-found when data hasn't been persisted yet.
- PageStore boundary tests: Account for AES-GCM overhead (28 bytes:
  12-byte nonce + 16-byte auth tag) in max payload calculations.

50e9688c5480b651969500a5470fbfc69ad7feef
2026-03-14
Michael Danylchuk
feat(wal): wire BlazeTransaction to DurabilityManager in unified mode
In unified mode, BlazeTransaction now uses DurabilityManager for
transaction boundaries (begin/commit/abort). Commit path enforces
WAL-before-page-write: encrypt → WAL writes → WAL commit (fsync) →
pwrite pages → fsync main. Single fsync per transaction (batch
optimization).

Rollback in unified mode discards staged pages without baseline
restore since pages are never written before commit.

PageStore gains _encryptPageBuffer and _writeEncryptedBuffer internal
methods to support the split encrypt/write flow.

TransactionContext branches legacy/unified paths. Legacy path unchanged.
7 new tests, 58 total tests green.

Phase 1, Chunk 3 Task 5 of WAL consolidation.

68d55015b6879eebf1f0a8d33db6f73d119cf8f7
2026-03-14
Michael Danylchuk
fix: WALEntry memory safety, error labels, and test coverage
Apply 7 critical fixes to WALEntry implementation:

1. Fix bindMemory UB in computeCRC32: Replace buf.bindMemory with
   buf.baseAddress!.assumingMemoryBound(to: UInt8.self) for correct
   alignment-free memory access per Swift UnsafeRawBufferPointer semantics.

2. Swap inverted CRC mismatch error labels: now correctly reports
   crcMismatch(expected: computed, actual: stored) per error semantics.

3. Add guard for offset < data.startIndex in readNext to prevent
   underflow on negative or invalid offsets.

4. Wrap computeCRC32 in #if canImport(zlib) with fatalError fallback
   since CRC32 is a hard requirement for WAL integrity.

5. Replace fragile string matching in CRC test with guard-case pattern
   matching on WALError.crcMismatch enum case.

6. Add testReadNextSequential to validate sequential entry decoding and
   offset tracking across multiple entries.

7. Add testZeroLSNRoundTrip to ensure LSN=0 boundary case works correctly.

All 6 tests pass (now includes 2 new tests). Build clean.

---COMMIT---
d671e996be969e5538feca7aba8b2a69ea7fd62b
2026-03-13
Michael Danylchuk
fix: use UUID instanceID for fetchAll cache key instead of ObjectIdentifier
ObjectIdentifier uses the memory address of DynamicCollection, which can
be reused after deallocation. When a new collection lands at the same
address, the 5-second TTL cache returns stale results from the previous
collection — causing insertMany records to be invisible to queries.

Fix: add a stable UUID instanceID to DynamicCollection, use it as the
cache dictionary key instead of ObjectIdentifier.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.6 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

b672bc914238e9f35cece2e9f81434956bdaca85
2026-03-09
Michael Danylchuk
Fix 4 test bugs in Tier 3 Destructive FailureInjectionTests
- Replace XCTAssertNoThrow({ closure }) with do/catch — the closure
  form creates a closure object (never throws) but never executes it,
  leaving self.db nil. Affects: testCorruptedMetadataRecovery,
  testMissingMetadataRecovery, testDetectPartialMetadataWrite.
- Fix testWrongPasswordFailsGracefully: use a password that meets
  strength requirements ("WrongTestDB-789!" instead of "wrong-password")
  and handle init throwing as a valid graceful-failure path.

---COMMIT---
42364d64d3c52eb3f8ecfc487362c44c31e79f51
2026-03-09
Michael Danylchuk
Fix 5 pre-existing bugs found during test tier validation
- RLS: Change userOwnsRecord/userInTeam policies from restrictive to
  permissive (they grant access, not restrict it). Split viewerReadOnly
  into restrictive deny-all + permissive select companion.
- Schema: Add createdAt to auto-injected fields skip set in strict mode
  (BlazeDBClient.insert() injects it automatically).
- Upsert: Fix TOCTOU race by catching BlazeDBError.recordExists on
  concurrent insert and retrying as update.
- Vacuum: Flush WAL/memory to disk before reading file size, preventing
  file-not-found when data hasn't been persisted yet.
- PageStore boundary tests: Account for AES-GCM overhead (28 bytes:
  12-byte nonce + 16-byte auth tag) in max payload calculations.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.6 <noreply@anthropic.com>

6a06b57bf5686b16957cc3e7f1c47a5f24a3546c
2026-03-09
Michael Danylchuk
Public API reduction: safety fixes, deprecations, error taxonomy, and convenience APIs
Phase 1 — Safety: Remove silent password injection, mark @Field macro stubs
unavailable, remove base64 auto-coercion, internalize RLS surface, resolve
health method collision.

Phase 2 — Deprecations: Bless 2 open paths (named:password:, at:password:),
deprecate init sprawl (openDefault, openForCLI, openForDaemon, openOrCreate,
create), deprecate *Async suffixed methods, explain sprawl, flush() alias.

Phase 3 — Taxonomy: Reclassify ~30 misused transactionFailed errors, fix
createIndex error messages for public API, remove 82 debug prints, route 11
operational signals through BlazeLogger, remove heuristic decoding (date/bool
field-name inference).

Phase 4 — Conveniences: Add fetchRequired(id:), exists(id:), update all
examples and docs to blessed API paths, write migration guide.

6dc57f15feaab9b05eb3ec944b4d6d9a262ccb92
2026-01-23
Michael Danylchuk
Pin SwiftCBOR to stable tagged release (v0.6.0)
SwiftPM requires stable transitive dependencies when consumers pin BlazeDB to a stable version.
Pinning SwiftCBOR to exact version 0.6.0 ensures reproducible builds and resolves SwiftPM dependency resolution errors.

This is required for production hygiene: when external apps pin BlazeDB @ 0.1.0, all transitive dependencies must also be stable.

- Phase 1: Created 3 test targets (Gate, Core, Legacy)

Tier 2 (Core) tests are important but non-blocking.
Tier 3 (Legacy) tests are internal/historical and never block releases.

This structure ensures CI green means production safety, not 'all tests pass'.

Implement hardening improvements: lifecycle safety, resource limits, format versioning
Hardening Phase Complete:

1. Process Lifecycle Safety:
   - Added explicit close() method with idempotency guarantees
   - Added isClosed property and ensureNotClosed() guards
   - Enhanced deinit to mark database as closed
   - Added signal-safe shutdown documentation

2. Single-Writer Enforcement:
   - Verified file locking in PageStore (already implemented)
   - Enhanced databaseLocked error messages with actionable guidance

3. Resource Bounds & Warnings:
   - Added DatabaseHealth.ResourceLimits configuration
   - Added resource limit checking (WAL, page count, disk usage)
   - Integrated warnings into health() reports
   - Optional write refusal when limits exceeded

4. On-Disk Compatibility Contract:
   - Added FormatVersion struct with semantic versioning
   - Format version stored in metadata for new databases
   - Format version validated at open time
   - Incompatible versions refused with actionable errors

All changes are at API boundary level - no frozen core modifications.
All tests pass. Swift 6 strict concurrency compliant.

  * DynamicCollection+Vector.swift (vector indexing)

  * DynamicCollection+MetaStore.swift (metadata optimizations)

- Apple platforms retain full feature set
- Linux gets core functionality only (CRUD, storage, BlazeBinary)
- No logic changes, pure platform gating

a37acdf9b1d5115d495dc9bb40ce786e281b4d07
2025-12-14
Michael Danylchuk
AUDIT: Harden multi-process file locking implementation
Phase 1 - Locking Semantics:
- Verified lock acquired before any writes possible
- Confirmed lock tied to same file descriptor used for I/O
- Validated lock lifetime (held until deinit/process exit)
- Verified same-process behavior (separate file descriptors)
- Confirmed crash safety (OS releases lock on process exit)

Phase 2 - Error Handling:
- Added errno checking to distinguish lock conflicts from system errors
- Lock conflicts (EWOULDBLOCK/EAGAIN) → databaseLocked
- System errors → permissionDenied
- Added return value checking for flock() release

Phase 3 - Test Coverage:
- Fixed testLockReleaseOnClose() to use deterministic retry (removed Thread.sleep)
- Hardened testSingleProcessReentrancy() to strictly require databaseLocked error
- Added testCrashSafety_LockReleaseOnProcessTermination() for crash safety verification
- All tests now deterministic (no sleeps, no timing hacks)

Phase 4 - Implementation Hygiene:
- Enhanced documentation in ARCHITECTURE.md with detailed locking information
- Added comprehensive code comments with preconditions/postconditions
- Verified POSIX usage (constants, imports, return values)

All critical issues resolved. Implementation is correct, crash-safe, and fully tested.

CRITICAL: Implement multi-process file locking
- Add exclusive POSIX file locking using flock() in PageStore
- Lock acquired on database open, released on close/deinit
- Prevents data corruption from concurrent multi-process writes
- Fail fast with BlazeDBError.databaseLocked if lock cannot be acquired
- Update error enum to include path parameter for better diagnostics
- Add invalidInput error case (was used but not defined)
- Update tests to verify exclusive locking behavior
- Update documentation to explain multi-process safety

This is a correctness fix to prevent silent data corruption when
multiple processes attempt to write to the same database file.

2025-12-07
Michael Danylchuk
Add comprehensive Cryptographic Architecture section to README
Added detailed cryptographic architecture documentation covering:

- High-level overview of two parallel cryptographic pipelines (data at rest and data in transit)
- Data at Rest: Complete local encryption pipeline with Argon2id → HKDF → AES-256-GCM flow, Secure Enclave integration, and page-level encryption details
- Data in Transit: ECDH handshake, session key derivation, frame encryption, and replay protection mechanisms
- Putting It Together: Explanation of key separation, lifecycle, and threat surface coverage

Includes professional Mermaid diagrams styled like internal infrastructure documentation, detailed key derivation flows, and clear security properties for each pipeline.

Updated Table of Contents and 'How to Read This Document' section to reference the new content.

---COMMIT---
cf22829f02db3fa2bf42da1c25a698f0abb7b76a
2025-11-21
Michael Danylchuk
Remove debug print statements and add input validation for database/collection names
Bug 91: Removed debug print() statements from BlazeDBClient.swift init error handling and converted to BlazeLogger calls for consistent logging.

Bug 92: Removed debug print() statements from DynamicCollection.swift init and converted to BlazeLogger calls.

Bug 93: Added input validation for database names, project names, and collection names to prevent path traversal attacks. Names are now validated to:
- Reject path traversal characters (../, ..\)
- Reject null bytes (\0)
- Require non-empty names
- Limit names to 255 characters

This prevents security vulnerabilities where malicious names could be used to access files outside the intended database directory.

M	BlazeDB/Core/BlazeDBManager.swift
M	BlazeDB/Core/DynamicCollection.swift
M	BlazeDB/Exports/BlazeDBClient.swift

---COMMIT---
9ebedcf47197ef1dd95cfdf813120c6d30675f0a
2025-11-21
Michael Danylchuk
Fix OperationPool release wake-up logic and remove debug print statements
Bug 1: OperationPool.release() was waking up waiting operations even when activeOperations was 0 (double-release case). This caused resource exhaustion and incorrect concurrency behavior. Fixed by moving wake-up logic inside the activeOperations > 0 block.

Bug 2: Removed all debug print() statements from insertBatch method and converted them to appropriate BlazeLogger calls (debug, info, warn, error) for consistent logging and better performance.

Release v1.0.0 - 100% Test Coverage Achievement
🎉 Major milestone: BlazeDB has achieved complete test coverage and stability!

New Features & Improvements:
- Comprehensive test suite with 100% passing tests
- Added BlazeDB-Package.xctestplan for integrated testing
- Complete TESTING_COVERAGE.md documentation

New Test Files:
- BlazeDataCRUDTests: Complete CRUD operation validation
- PageStoreEdgeCaseTests: Storage edge case handling
- StorageStatsTests: Storage statistics and metrics
- TransactionDurabilityTests: Transaction persistence validation
- TransactionEdgeCaseTests: Transaction boundary conditions
- TransactionRecoveryTests: Recovery and rollback scenarios

Core Enhancements:
- Improved BlazeCollection with enhanced index support
- Enhanced BlazeDBManager for multi-database management
- Refined DynamicCollection with better type safety
- Optimized BlazeQuery DSL with chainable operations
- Enhanced PageStore with better memory management
- Improved StorageLayout for efficient data organization
- Robust BlazeTransaction with full ACID compliance
- Enhanced TransactionContext and TransactionLog

All systems validated and production-ready!
